# Ash-backed TCG Engine Playtest Handoff

- Updated: 2026-07-07 (superseded by latest-Limitless/Godot roadmap)
- Sources: Project codebase; local validation; wiki log
- Raw: N/A — operational handoff

- This document is historical/operational context. The active canonical roadmap is now [Prizmo TCG Engine and Play Surface North Star](ash-backed-tcg-engine-playtest-north-star.md): Goal 1 latest-Limitless Dragapult/Alakazam completeness, Goal 2 latest top-30 Limitless archetype coverage, Goal 3 React shell + embedded Godot play surface, Goal 4 React Native mobile path, Goal 5 all possible cards.
- Treat the two-deck and six-deck selection rules below as historical unless they directly help with the current latest-Limitless coverage goals.

## [2026-07-07] Goal 2 Relicanth support batch

- Current state: `TEF-084` Relicanth is now closed instead of staying as the default highest-share shared unimplemented Goal 2 card after Pokémon Catcher. Added committed TCGdex metadata and executable Ash engine behavior as a Basic Fighting Pokémon: `Razor Fin` is plain 30 damage, and `Memory Dive` is modeled as a passive in-play Ability that lets the owner's evolved Active Pokémon use executable attacks from previous Evolutions while preserving normal Energy costs, attack locks, attack restrictions, declaration/resolution, and `GameView` affordance behavior through the shared `AttackAccess` path.
- Current live snapshot: rerunning the live Goal 2 corpus after the batch keeps the same `377` tracked cards but moves the support buckets to `supported=186`, `generic-supported=8`, `partial=10`, and `unimplemented=173`, while the metadata split improves to `cached=206` / `missing=171`. Cynthia's Garchomp ex and Archaludon ex no longer list `TEF-084`; the highest-share remaining shared unimplemented card is now `MEG-129` Surfing Beach.
- Validation: TCGdex `sv05-084` endpoint confirmed Relicanth metadata and printed text; `mix format`; `mix test test/prizmo/tcg_engine/mechanics_test.exs --only describe:"Goal 2 metadata-backed support slice"`; `mix test test/prizmo/tcg/cards/metadata_test.exs`; `mix compile --warnings-as-errors`; `mix test test/prizmo/tcg/cards/metadata_test.exs test/prizmo/tcg_engine/mechanics_test.exs`; `mix prizmo.goal2.corpus`; final `mix check` passed.
- Next recommendation: take `MEG-129` Surfing Beach next as the highest-share remaining shared unimplemented card (`2` archetypes / `0.70%` total share) unless the live corpus re-ranks the queue. Keep `CRI-079` Philippe and the `BLK-067` Genesect ex / `DRI-164` Energy Recycler tied pair as immediate shared follow-ups by weighted share.

## [2026-07-07] Goal 2 Neo Upper Energy support batch

- Current state: `TEF-162` Neo Upper Energy is now closed instead of staying as the default highest-share shared unimplemented Goal 2 card after Iris's Fighting Spirit. Added committed TCGdex metadata and executable Ash engine behavior as an ACE SPEC Special Energy: it provides one Colorless Energy while attached to non-Stage 2 Pokémon, provides two units of any supported basic Energy type while attached to Stage 2 Pokémon, and pays two-type Stage 2 attack costs such as Dragapult ex `Phantom Dive` from a single attached copy. The generic Energy attachment path now also enforces ACE SPEC availability before attachment, marks ACE SPEC usage after attaching an ACE SPEC Energy, and keeps later ACE SPEC plays rejected server-side.

## [2026-07-07] Goal 2 Iris's Fighting Spirit support batch

- Current state: `JTG-149` Iris's Fighting Spirit is now closed instead of staying as the default highest-share shared unimplemented Goal 2 card after Hassel. Added committed TCGdex metadata and executable Ash engine behavior as an Item: playing the card counts the acting player's Prize cards, shuffles that Prize stack to the bottom of that player's deck using the persisted trainer-effect RNG context, then places the same number of cards from the top of the deck face down as replacement Prize cards.

## [2026-07-07] Goal 2 Powerglass support batch

- Current state: `SFA-063` Powerglass is now closed instead of staying as the default highest-share remaining shared Goal 2 Tool blocker after Metagross. Added committed TCGdex metadata and authored it as an engine-defined Tool effect that creates an optional end-of-turn select-cards prompt when attached to the active player's Active Pokémon and Basic Energy exists in that player's discard pile. The prompt is available both after passing without attacking and after attack resolution before the turn finishes, attaches the chosen Basic Energy from discard to the attached Active Pokémon, allows selecting no Energy, marks the source Tool so a declined prompt is not immediately repeated, and is suppressed while Jamming Tower makes Pokémon Tools have no effect.

It is a good next batch if the choice system can encode its exclusive `up to 2 Basic Pokémon or 1 Evolution Pokémon` rule cleanly; otherwise move to the still-broad cached cards with simpler semantics such as `TEF-157` Prime Catcher, `JTG-121` Dudunsparce ex, or `SCR-114` Hoothoot.

## [2026-06-23] Goal 1 Last-Ditch Catch trigger fix + `28259` / `28385` seeded validation

- Current state: seeded browser replay `38102f61-bcb3-46fc-9680-1b1022cdb113` for live Dragapult `28259` versus live Alakazam `28385` with `goal1-28259-vs-28385-seed-101` added the missing mid-game validation signal for the last unproven Dragapult latest-result id and one of the last unproven Alakazam ids. The line now reaches player-2 Turn 2 with player-1 `DRI-180` Team Rocket's Watchtower active, `POR-062` Meowth ex on Bench, `JTG-120` Dunsparce Active for player 2, and a clean post-Hilda action window for player 2 with no console errors.
- Current state follow-up: this replay surfaced and closed a real canonical Goal 1 engine gap. `POR-062` Meowth ex had committed card text and registry coverage for `Last-Ditch Catch`, but `Mechanics.play_basic_to_bench/3` never triggered the Ability. Benching Meowth on Turn 1 consumed the card from hand and wrote `play_basic_to_bench`, but no prompt opened and no Supporter entered hand. The fix added an automatic bench-triggered `ability_effect` prompt path plus resolution in `AbilityEffects`: when `Meowth ex` is played from hand to Bench, the engine now opens a `last ditch catch` select-cards prompt over legal Supporters in deck, moving the chosen Supporter to hand with public reveal, shuffling the deck with engine-owned RNG context, writing a public `ability_used` note, and enforcing the printed once-per-turn `Last-Ditch` limiter across the acting player's cards through a shared marker. Replaying the same seeded game after the fix confirmed the browser prompt with 8 legal Supporter choices and public event history lines `Meowth ex used Last-Ditch Catch.` / `Meowth ex revealed 1 card.` after choosing `Lillie's Determination`.
- Current state follow-up: the same replay also added new passive/Supporter validation signal without surfacing further bugs. After player 1 played `DRI-180` Team Rocket's Watchtower and passed, player-2 Turn 2 opened with Colorless `Dunsparce` Active but no `Run Away Draw` affordance, which is the expected browser/read-model result under Watchtower's Ability suppression. `WHT-084` Hilda still appeared normally, opened the mixed Evolution-plus-Energy search prompt with 13 legal choices, resolved `Kadabra` + `Enriching Energy`, shuffled, and returned to the action window.
- Validation: browser replay on `http://localhost:4003`; supported game `38102f61-bcb3-46fc-9680-1b1022cdb113`; zero Playwright console errors; direct Tidewave read-model verification that `GameView.for_player/2` on player 2 with Watchtower active exposes affordance keys only `play_card`, `play_basic_to_bench`, `attach_energy`, and `pass` (no `run_away_draw`), and that player 1's private hand after the Meowth prompt includes the searched `Lillie's Determination`; focused `mix test test/prizmo/tcg_engine/mechanics_test.exs --only line:839 --only line:919` and final `mix check` pass.
- Next recommendation: `28259` and `28385` now have meaningful seeded browser signal, so the highest-value remaining Goal 1 validation work is the other unproven Alakazam latest-result ids `28398` and `27615`. Favor seeds that exercise still-unproven prompt-heavy or passive states such as `TWM-158` Lucky Helmet, `PFL-085` Battle Cage follow-up, extra `POR-088` lines, or other mid-game states that go beyond setup/pass.

The same runtime probe also exposed an Eri resolution bug where `require_all_item_cards/1` passed `{:ok, metadata}` tuples into `collect_ok_results/1`; resolving the prompt through the browser now succeeds and returns to the turn action window.
- Current state follow-up: the `28255` versus `28291` Ciphermaniac run exposed and closed another real bug in the canonical Goal 1 play surface. `TEF-145` uses an unfiltered `:search_deck` effect that puts the selected cards on top of the deck; the read-model helper crashed on the missing `filter`, and the resolver had no `:deck_top` destination implementation. `CardPlay` now treats nil search filters as legal, `CardStore.move_deck_cards_to_top/3` preserves chosen order on top of deck, and the browser replay resolves the full prompt back to the action window. The `28261` versus `28431` Pokégear run also validated the `SVI-186` no-hit top-7 Supporter prompt path and empty-choice resolution.

The first browser replay after the engine batch exposed another real canonical-path gap: `GameView.ActionAffordances` listed `Use Fan Call`, but the SPA had no executable button because `Prizmo.TcgEngine` had not exposed the new Ash action through `typescript_rpc` / code-interface wiring and `index.tsx` had no `fan_call` renderer. The follow-up batch added the RPC/client/SPA command path, replayed Turn 2 `Fan Call` prompt creation/resolution in browser, and then fixed a second defect surfaced by the same run: the engine incorrectly offered `Fan Call` again on player-2 Turn 4. `AbilityEffects.require_fan_call_available/2` now rejects later turns with `:fan_call_only_available_on_first_turn`, and the replayed Turn 4 browser state no longer shows the command.
- Current state follow-up: seeded browser replay `1afd27a4-ae9e-4e4e-aa88-3e594ee5eba2` for Dragapult `28264` versus Alakazam `28405` with `goal1-28264-vs-28405-twm082-935` exposed and closed another canonical Goal 1 rules bug while trying to reach `TWM-082` Alakazam lines. On player-2 Turn 2, the browser initially exposed `Play Rare Candy`, opened a `rare_candy_evolve_basic_to_stage_2` prompt for `Abra` plus `TWM-082` Alakazam, and resolved the illegal Stage 2 evolution even though it was still player 2's first turn. The fix now applies evolution timing to the acting player's actual first turn rather than only `turn_number > 1`, and threads that rule through generic `evolve_from_hand`, Rare Candy choice generation/validation, and `GameView.ActionAffordances`. Replaying the same seed after the fix reached the identical Turn 2 state with no `Play Rare Candy` affordance, and direct Tidewave verification showed `Requirements.require_evolution_allowed_this_turn/2` returning `{:error, :cannot_evolve_on_first_turn}` for `{2, "player_2"}` while a direct `Mechanics.play_card/3` retry on the in-hand `MEG-125` now rejects with zero legal Rare Candy choices.
- Current state follow-up: the same `28264` versus `28405` seeded game is now continued past that corrected Turn 2 state, which closes the exact browser-validation follow-up the previous handoff recommended. Player-2 Turn 2 attached `POR-088` Telepathic Psychic Energy to `Abra`, resolved the attach-trigger prompt with mixed `Abra` + `Elgyem` deck choices, passed to player-1 Turn 3, and then absorbed `Petty Grudge` so player-2 Turn 4 opened on an energized damaged `Abra`. On that legal turn, the browser exposed `Play Rare Candy`, opened the `rare_candy_evolve_basic_to_stage_2` prompt with exactly the two valid in-play `Abra` targets, evolved the active `Abra` into `TWM-082` Alakazam with `POR-088` still attached, and immediately exposed both `Psychic` and `Strange Hacking`. The replay then chose `Strange Hacking`; with no damage counters on the opponent's board, the attack resolved through the no-redistribution path, Confused the opponent's Active `Dreepy`, advanced to player-1 Turn 5, and produced no browser console errors.
- Current state follow-up: seeded browser replay `1d559bbd-2529-448a-a0ea-eb583a364890` for Dragapult `27611` versus Alakazam `28368` with `goal1-27611-vs-28368-depth-608` added a new no-bug validation slice rather than surfacing another fix. The browser exposed `PFL-087` Dawn, resolved its staged search prompt for `Abra` + `Kadabra` + `Alakazam`, then proved an important passive-plus-prompt chain on the live play surface: the searched `Abra` could be benched from hand into opposing `MEG-127` Risky Ruins for 20 damage, `POR-088` could then attach to that damaged Abra and open its attach-trigger prompt with four legal Basic Psychic choices, and resolving that prompt with mixed `Abra` + `Dedenne` choices benched both cards from deck and shuffled cleanly before player 2 replaced the Stadium with `PFL-085` Battle Cage and passed. The repo now also has `mix prizmo.goal1.seed_search` for precomputing promising fixture-mode opening hands before opening the browser.

## [2026-06-02] Iteration 243 handoff — complete gameplay correction + Budew Item lock

- Historical 2026-06-02 direction: complete gameplay of all six target meta decks was the north star at that time. The durable lesson still applies: do **not** treat a card registry entry, behavior overlay, partial coverage inventory, or empty documented blocker list as completion. If a target-deck card still appears as `Pending card text` on the live board, or a real deck line cannot execute its printed gameplay through Ash/React, roadmap work is not done.
- Completed this batch: Budew (`ASC-016`) from Dragapult (`27431`) moved off pending attack text. Its `Itchy Pollen` attack now has an authored ASC behavior overlay with effect `:lock_opponent_items_next_turn`, a Pokémon CardDefinition in `EngineCardRegistry`, Ash attack support in `AttackEffects`/`AttackDamage`, and durable Item-lock legality in `Prizmo.TcgEngine.ItemLocks`. Resolving the attack records viewer-safe lock payload on `resolve_declared_attack`; during the blocked player's next turn, generic `play_card` Item use and older direct Item helpers reject via `:items_locked_by_itchy_pollen` and the affordance read model hides playable Items.
- Still open from the screenshot: Munkidori (`TWM-095`) `Adrena-Brain` remains a six-deck target gameplay gap. It has legacy sim behavior but no Ash Ability action surface yet. The correct next slice is a real Ability command/prompt path: once-per-turn gate, Darkness Energy attachment requirement, choose own damaged Pokémon, choose opponent in-play Pokémon, choose up to 3 counters, move counters, write public event payloads, resolve KOs/prizes/replacement Active, expose the command in GameView/React, and keep hidden information safe.
- Selection rule: prefer target-deck visible pending gameplay text and scenario-blocking mechanics over UI polish. Next high-value batches should continue Dragapult pending text (`TWM-095` Adrena-Brain and any other current-board pending target cards), then refresh a six-deck live-playability inventory that checks actual GameView/React pending text instead of only registry coverage.

## North-star reset handoff (historical — six-deck milestone)

- Historical direction (now superseded): [Six-Deck Fully Playable TCG Engine North Star](ash-backed-tcg-engine-playtest-north-star.md) was used as the first broad milestone before scope narrowed to Dragapult-versus-Alakazam two-deck execution.
- Historical selection rule for autonomous agents: choose six-deck card coverage inventory, target-deck behavior implementation, or target-deck playability validation before UI polish, Electric Streams, broad open-deck expansion, AI, coaching, renderer work, or non-target deck authoring. If a task does not reduce six-deck blockers, defer it unless the user explicitly overrides that reset.
- Target decks: `27431` Dragapult, `27147` Alakazam, `27599` Raging Bolt Ogerpon, `27445` Festival Lead, `27514` Lopunny Dudunsparce, and `27459` Rocket Mewtwo.
- Definition of fully playable: every card in those decks must have canonical Ash engine behavior or a deliberate generic behavior path sufficient for normal play. Unsupported target-deck behavior is a blocker. Generic fallback is acceptable for truly generic primitives such as Basic Energy/plain damage/already-standardized attachment and play mechanics; it is not acceptable for printed target-deck effects that affect gameplay.
- Selection rule for validation: game `f6df7025-7d0f-4d9b-9bc2-31c864de1d4e`, preseeded fixtures, deterministic seeds, and narrow scripted scenarios remain valid when they prove engine correctness, prevent regressions, or validate a six-deck card behavior path. They should not be mistaken for complete deck playability by themselves.
- Required first implementation task after this reset: produce or refresh a canonical Ash-engine coverage inventory for all unique cards in the six target deck modules. Classify cards as `engine-defined`, `generic-supported`, `partial`, or `blocker`, and use that inventory to pick the next blocker.
- UI batches remain subject to the existing benchmark rule before substantial layout changes, but UI polish is now deferred unless it blocks six-deck validation. Batch 219 completed the current benchmark baseline.
- Keep raw payloads, IDs, debug counters, and tutorial copy out of the normal play path. The product target is a serious, dense card table for players who already know Pokémon TCG.

React SPA additions: `promptSubmitLabel` shows "Apply +30 damage bonus (no switch)" at 0 selection or "Switch to selected Bench Pokémon X/Y" with cards; `promptGuidanceMessages` explains the choice; `kieranPromptFlowGuide` provides a three-step flow guide. - Completed first in-game RNG Trainer slice: Crushing Hammer (`POR-071`) is now an engine-defined Item using the generic Ash engine `play_card` path. The server flips the coin itself when the Item is played, using persisted RNG metadata when `rng_seed` is present and falling back to process RNG otherwise. A public `coin_flipped` event note now reports the result without exposing `rng_seed`. On heads, the engine opens `discard_opponent_attached_energy_if_heads` over one opponent attached Energy, exposes that public attached card in the viewer prompt payload, discards the selected Energy on resolution, and completes through the normal effect/card-play sequence. On tails, the Item discards itself and completes immediately with no prompt and no opponent Energy discard. `GameView` now treats Crushing Hammer as engine-defined instead of pending Trainer text, and the React prompt/action guidance explains the flip-first, heads-only discard path.
